# Taxonomy follow-up research report
Corpus: 161 projects / 710 compartments / 22,557 nodes / 31,826 edges (2026-10-14). Registry: 1,510 nodes / 72 distinct data-type ids (2026-10-14).
## 1. Data-type usage map
Generated by `analysis/t3_datatype_usage.py`; re-running replaces this section in place (rest of `report.md` untouched). Raw tables: `analysis/t3_table.md`; unresolved tokens: `analysis/unresolved_types.md`.

Method: each corpus edge carries the raw on-disk `slot-data-type` token of its source output slot (0 of 31,826 edges carry a null token). Tokens are resolved against the registry in the order exact data-type id → exact class FQN → simple class name (an ambiguous simple-name match is recorded, not guessed); unresolved tokens go to `analysis/unresolved_types.md`. Producer/consumer node ids are resolved project-wide (edges are re-homed into the target compartment; `fromUid` may point at a node in another compartment). “Registry … (capability)” = distinct registered node ids declaring that data type on an output/input slot; “corpus … (edge count)” = the same counted over corpus edges, i.e. what pipelines actually wire. All tables sorted by edge count descending.

## 2. Workflow chains
Generated by `analysis/t4_workflows.py`; re-running replaces this section in place (rest of `report.md` untouched). Raw tables: `analysis/t4_table.md`.

Method:
- **2a — top-20 node ids:** aggregated by registry id across the corpus: distinct projects (a node used twice in one project counts once) + total node instances; sorted by project count desc, then instance count desc, then id.
- **2b/2c — chains:** a chain A→B→C is two data-class-typed edges e1 (A→B) and e2 (B→C) with e1.toUid == e2.fromUid (the same B node instance); counted per occurrence, table rows deduped by the (A.id, B.id, C.id) id-triple, top 20 by occurrence count (deterministic tie-break: occurrences desc, then lexicographic triple). ONE project-wide uid→node map is built per project before any graph walk (edges are re-homed into the target compartment: 4,661 of 31,826 edges have a fromUid in another compartment). Edge `dataClass` = the source slot's raw on-disk data-type token: 0 of 31,826 edges carry a null token and 929 carry one of the 8 unresolved tokens of Task 3 (kept as their own token in the chain statistics — never dropped); 0 typed edges with an unresolved fromUid and 0 (e1, e2) pairs with an unresolved toUid were skipped (0 / 0 here — nothing dropped).
- **Branch facets (2c):** each node's branch set = the union of the top-level branches of ALL its function leaves (primary + secondary, registry `functions[]`), each leaf's `parent` walked up the registry function tree (88 functions, 13 top-level branches; verified acyclic). **Fragmented** = the three nodes' branch sets span ≥ 3 distinct top-level branches. **Isolated middle** = B's primary branch (top-level of its primary function) is in neither A's nor C's branch set; in 30 triples (238 occurrences) the middle node's id is absent from the registry (legacy plugin ids, see below) so no primary resolves to a branch — their isolated-middle flag is reported as 'no' (not computable, not a negative signal).
- **2d — dead-end candidates (interpretation, recorded verbatim):** “frequently consumed” = node id with ≥ 25 typed outgoing edges across the whole corpus; “output type's consumers all in one narrow branch” = take the node's OUTPUT data-type ids (registry `outputs[].dataType`, non-null), the registry node ids that accept any of those as `inputs[].dataType`, and their branch sets (primary + secondary, as above); flag when that consumer branch union is exactly 1 top-level branch AND that branch ≠ the node's own primary branch. These are “candidates for secondary-function assignments or family steps” (the WHY is Task 9's job — counts + ids + the one branch name only).

Summary: 4111 distinct id-triples with ≥1 occurrence (85,925 total occurrences); 1444 fragmented triples overall (3 of the top 20); 3 dead-end candidates.

Corpus node ids absent from the 6.0.0 registry that carry typed outgoing edges (6 of the 9 absent ids; legacy plugin ids — no function leaves, treated as an empty branch set): `clij:plugin:net.haesleinhuepf.clij2.plugins.ErodeLabels` (6), `clij:plugin:net.haesleinhuepf.clij2.plugins.ExtendedDepthOfFocusSobelProjection` (214), `clij:plugin:net.haesleinhuepf.clij2.plugins.ReduceLabelsToCentroids` (2), `clij:plugin:net.haesleinhuepf.clij2.plugins.ReduceLabelsToLabelEdges` (4), `clij:plugin:net.haesleinhuepf.clijx.clij2wrappers.ExtendedDepthOfFocusSobelProjection` (10), `plotly-table-to-histogram` (2).

## 3. Families vs reality
Generated by `analysis/t5_families.py`; re-running replaces this section in place (rest of `report.md` untouched). Raw tables: `analysis/t5_table.md`.

Method:
- **Definitions:** a family's **member set** = the set of `nodeId` values across its `registry.json` `families[].steps[]` (trackmate: 21 distinct node ids from 21 steps; plotting: 1). A **declared after-edge** of family F = (F, B, A) where B is a step's `nodeId` and A is in B's `after[]` (the resolved predecessor node ids; trackmate: 20 edges, plotting: 0). **ONE project-wide uid→node map is built per project before any walk** (edges are re-homed into the target compartment). Of 31,826 corpus edges: 518 are between members of the same family (380 wiring a declared after-edge, 138 wiring an undeclared member pair), 31,308 are between non-member or cross-family nodes; the typed tokens are 30,897 resolvable to a registry data type and 929 not (null: 0).
- **3a — declared vs real:** a declared after-edge (B, A) is **wired** when ≥ 1 corpus edge has source node id A and target node id B (ANY `dataClass` — a real connection is a real connection; occurrences and distinct projects counted per family). Otherwise **declared-but-never-wired**. **Undeclared-real** edges: corpus edges (A→B) whose two node ids are both in F's member set but where B's `after[]` does not contain A — real workflow edges the family graph is missing (top 20 by occurrence; all listed in `analysis/t5_table.md`).
- **3b — family candidates:** corpus edges (A→B) with A.id != B.id, node(A).framework == node(B).framework (non-null; `jipipe:framework:none` excluded — 5,757 such edges) where the pair is NOT connected by any declared after-edge in ANY family, in either direction (380 same-framework edges excluded for this reason). Computed over ALL corpus edges (per the ruling's mechanical rule, not restricted to cross-family edges): 138 of the qualifying occurrences are same-family edges that are also reported as undeclared-real in 3a (the trackmate `mask-detector→detector` chain) — the overlap is intentional, not an error (reading 3b restricted to pairs where NEITHER endpoint is a family member: 803 pairs / 10,684 occurrences). Frameworks absent from the corpus are absent from the results by construction. 13,150 edges had different frameworks and never qualify.
- **3c — specialized data types:** the **low-consumer set is RECOMPUTED** here (t3's markdown not parsed for the set, t3 not re-run): a registry data type T (a non-null `outputs[].dataType`) with exactly 1-2 DISTINCT consumer node ids, where consumer = a registry node id observed as the target of a corpus edge whose resolved token is T (t3's counting rule, mirrored). **Cross-check: AGREE** — the recomputed set (11 types) equals the registry-known rows of the committed `analysis/t3_table.md` Table 1c flagged sub-table (11 of its 15 rows; 4 rows are unresolved raw tokens, not registry data types, so the recompute rule — `T` is a non-null `outputs[].dataType` — excludes them by definition). For each T, the **producer framework(s)** = the distinct `framework` of registry nodes that OUTPUT T (registry capability, unlike the corpus-observed consumers); a framework **HAS families** when ≥ 1 node with that framework is in any family's member set. T is (i) **with-family** when any producer framework HAS families (the trackmate case) or (ii) **without-family** otherwise (the siblings — narrow-consumer specialized types with no family to anchor them).

Step 2 verification (computed from `registry.json`): the trackmate family has 21 steps with 21 distinct member node ids (expected 21; the 7 `trackmate-create-spot-detector-*` nodes each have their own step) and 20 declared after-edges (expected 20); `trackmate-tracking.after` = the 7 detector ids + `trackmate-detector` (8 ids, expected 8); the plotting family has 1 step and 0 declared after-edges (expected 1 step, 0 edges). PASS

## 4. Function tree fit
Generated by `analysis/t6_functions.py`; re-running replaces this section in place (rest of `report.md` untouched). Raw tables (incl. full 4a occupancy): `analysis/t6_table.md`.

Method:
- **Branch definitions:** a node's **branch** = the top-level branch of its **primary** function (`functions[0]`; parent-walk to `null` — verified acyclic). A node's **secondary branches** = the top-level branch of each of its secondary functions (`functions[1:]`), deduped, excluding the primary branch. 13 top-level branches; 88 functions.
- **4a:** nodes counted per **primary function id** (leaf or branch — `misc` itself, `segmentation` and `scripting` can be primary values; 3 non-empty primary ids are branch-level, not leaves); the 19 functions never a primary are omitted. Top 15 biggest and bottom 15 smallest non-empty; the catch-all `misc` and its branch members are marked.
- **4b (dual reading):** (i) **catch-all only** — corpus-USED nodes whose primary function is exactly `jipipe:function:misc` (0 of them); (ii) **whole misc branch** — corpus-USED nodes whose primary function is in the misc subtree: `misc`, `data-utilities`, `scripting` + its 4 `scripting-*` leaf children, `text-markup` (9 of them, computed by branch-walk). Note: the ground-facts summary names 3 child leaves of `misc` and counts `scripting` itself as one of them (2 nodes, 0 used — matching the file's 2 nodes with primary `scripting` exactly); in the file `scripting` is additionally an intermediate node with 4 further leaf children (`scripting-expression`, `scripting-imagej-macro`, `scripting-python`, `scripting-r`), so the ruling's literal set {`misc`, `data-utilities`, `scripting`, `text-markup`} yields 7 used nodes while the whole-branch walk yields 9 — the difference is `cpython-script-iterating`, `external-imagej-macro` (their primaries are `scripting-*` leaves under `scripting`). The whole-branch walk is reported as reading (ii) (it is the 'whole misc branch'); the literal-set count is stated alongside.
- **4c:** distinct corpus-USED node ids per top-level primary branch; merge/demotion candidates = branches with < 3 used nodes (`statistics`); 0-used branches are the strongest candidates: `statistics`.
- **4d (candidate rule, verbatim):** for each corpus-USED node N with primary branch Pb and ≥ 1 secondary branch: gather N's corpus edges (in AND out, project-wide uid resolution, ANY dataClass; self-edges excluded; endpoints that are absent ids or have no primary branch ignored — 9 of 336 corpus-USED node ids are absent from the registry and treated as unknown-branch: `clij:plugin:net.haesleinhuepf.clij2.plugins.ErodeLabels`, `clij:plugin:net.haesleinhuepf.clij2.plugins.ExtendedDepthOfFocusSobelProjection`, `clij:plugin:net.haesleinhuepf.clij2.plugins.ReduceLabelsToCentroids`, `clij:plugin:net.haesleinhuepf.clij2.plugins.ReduceLabelsToLabelEdges`, `clij:plugin:net.haesleinhuepf.clijx.clij2wrappers.ExtendedDepthOfFocusSobelProjection`, `graph-annotation-group-box`, `plotly-export`, `plotly-table-to-animation-scatter`, `plotly-table-to-histogram`). Let mass(B) = number of N's neighbor-endpoints whose primary branch == B. primaryMass = mass(Pb); bestSecondaryBranch = argmax over N's secondary branches of mass (tie → lexicographically smallest); secondaryMass = mass(bestSecondaryBranch). N is a secondary-value candidate iff secondaryMass > primaryMass (strict) AND N has ≥ 3 neighbor-endpoints with known primary branches (evidence floor, to suppress noise). Top 20 shown, of 4 total candidates, over 31,826 corpus edges; 10 corpus-USED registry nodes have ≥ 1 secondary branch (denominator context; full list in `analysis/t6_table.md`).

## 5. Mined patterns vs the hand-written library
Generated by `analysis/t7_patterns.py`; re-running replaces this section in place (rest of `report.md` untouched). Raw tables: `analysis/t7_table.md`; machine-readable inventory: `patterns/patterns.json`.

Method:
- **Role signature (concrete, first match wins):** `import` = primary function's top-level branch is `jipipe:function:data`; `annotate` = branch `jipipe:function:annotations` (a function branch, not a framework); `export` = branch `jipipe:function:export`; `trackmate` = framework `jipipe:framework:trackmate`; `plotting` = branch `jipipe:function:plotting`; `core-io` = id in {`io-interface`, `jipipe:compartment-input`, `jipipe:compartment-output`}; `unknown` = absent from the registry or no primary function; else `other`. Order: import, annotate, export, trackmate, plotting, core-io, other. Role counts over the 336 distinct corpus-used node ids: annotate=27, core-io=2, export=7, import=22, other=247, plotting=7, trackmate=15, unknown=9. 9 corpus node ids are absent from the registry (legacy) and are treated as `unknown` (counted, never crashed on).
- **support** = number of DISTINCT projects in which the pattern occurs >= 1 time (deduped per project). **Cutoff: keep support >= 3**; 13,302 distinct patterns (support 1-2) were rejected. **occurrences** (where a class defines it) = total realizations (chains/conversions: edge-walks; fan-out/fan-in: qualifying node instances; boundary: edge count; sandwich: import->annotate->processor triples).
- **Class 1 — chains (`shape: chain`):** every maximal node-id sequence of length 3-5 along TYPED edges (both hops' `dataClass` non-null) in a project's project-wide node space; a chain is one id-tuple (occurrences = edge-paths realizing it). Overlaps Task 4's top-20 3-chains (this is the generalized multi-length inventory).
- **Class 2 — fan-out / fan-in (`shape: fan-out` / `fan-in`):** fan-out = a node X with >= 2 TYPED output edges to >= 2 DISTINCT target node ids; generalized signature = (X.id, sorted role-multiset of targets). Fan-in = dual (>= 2 typed input edges from >= 2 distinct sources).
- **Class 3 — conversions (`shape: conversion`):** typed 2-hop A->B->C where the data type changes at EVERY hop (`dataClass(A->B) != dataClass(B->C)`; null edges excluded); generalized by (A.id, B.id, C.id).
- **Class 4 — annotation sandwiches (`shape: annotation-sandwich`):** a node S of role `annotate` with >= 1 incoming edge (typed or any) from a role `import` node AND >= 1 outgoing edge to a node of role NOT in {import, annotate, unknown} (a 'processor'); generalized by (import_id, annotate_id, processor_role).
- **Class 5 — compartment motifs:** **boundary** (`compartment-boundary`) = inter-compartment edges (endpoints in different compartments per the uid->compartment map), generalized by (source_node_id, target_node_id), occurrences = edge count, keep support >= 3. **cycle** (`compartment-cycle`) = per-compartment DFS back-edge detection, generalized by (compartment name + node count), NO support cutoff (reported as M; JIPipe dataflow is a DAG).
- **Cross-check (Step 2):** a hand-written doc's named ids = every backtick-quoted token (extracted **per line** — `[^`]+` over the full text spans newlines and, in any doc with a multi-line code/mermaid block, pairs a fence's opening backtick with the next one, emitting one giant token that contains the real ids but is never an exact id, so they would be silently lost) that is an exact registry node id (distinct ids; the overlap denominator). A doc matches a mined pattern when >= 50% of its ids appear in the pattern's full nodeIds union (across all role groups); the best match (highest overlap, tie -> lowest pattern id) is recorded as `crossRefs.llmPatternDoc` + `crossRefs.llmOverlap`. **5b correction candidates** = docs with >= 2 extracted ids, NO pattern at overlap >= 50%, and not all ids corpus-absent. **5c library gaps** = mined patterns with support >= 5 and no doc at overlap >= 50%. Docs with < 2 extracted registry ids are 'unmatchable (few named ids)'.
- **patterns.json emission cap:** `patterns.json` emits only patterns with **support >= 20 OR the top-100 by support within each of the 5 conceptual classes** (class 2 = fan-out+fan-in; class 5 = boundary+cycle; union, sorted by support desc then id); the full statistics for ALL mined patterns remain in this report (5a). The literal 'support >= 5' rule would emit ~5,708 patterns / ~15 MB (the long chain tail), far over the ~3 MB budget, and support >= 18 still lands ~3.75 MB; support >= 20 + top-100/class is the highest-support set that fits (this run: 576 patterns, 1.6 MB). To keep the file small, each emitted pattern's `projects` list is **capped to the first 20 (sorted)** with a new `projectCount` = the true total; `support` always = the true distinct-project count and `exampleGraph` is kept in full (small; needed by Task 8). The long tail is represented numerically in the top-level `summary` object (`totalMined`, `byShape`, `emitted`, `supportHistogram` 1-2/3-4/>=5) — no silent data loss.
- **exampleGraph** = the smallest (fewest nodes; tie -> lexicographic project file + compartment name) subgraph with one occurrence: node ids + slot edges, parameters elided (the corpus carries no parameters).

## 6. Pattern library
Generated by `analysis/t8_pattern_docs.py`; re-running replaces this section in place (sections 1-5 untouched, scripts frozen). Docs: `patterns/<pattern-id>.md`; template `data` JSON: `patterns/templates/<pattern-id>.json`; raw 6a rows: `analysis/t8_table.md`. The end-to-end deserialization check of one template `data` (candidate `fan-in-ij1-roi-to-rgb-core-io-1-other-1`) lives in `jipipe-launcher/.../taxonomy/TemplateDataDeserializationTest.java` (JUnit, same module as `RegistryDumperTest`): **PASS** — `3` nodes (== the template's `3` nodes; the silent-empty case is ruled out), `1` connection (== the template's `1` edge; soft-skipped edges ruled out); every loaded node id is a registry id.

Method:
- **Curation rule (R8):** one doc per pattern that is (a) graph-shaped (`chain`/`conversion`/`fan-out`/`fan-in`) with support >= 25, OR (b) an annotation-sandwich (all 4, regardless of support). The plan's 'one doc per pattern, support >= 3' is overridden: 13,122 docs is a data dump, not a library. This run: **185 docs** (181 graph-shaped >= 25 + 4 sandwiches). The long tail (the sub-25 patterns, incl. support 3-4) stays numerically preserved in `patterns/patterns.json` `summary` + section 5a and is NOT documented.
- **Honest frontmatter (R8):** each doc keeps the LLM-branch frontmatter shape (`audience`, `modality`, `tags`, `biologicalContext`, `prerequisiteKnowledge`) plus a `corpus` block (support, top-5 projects, the example subgraph, `templateCandidate: true`) and a `template` block (proposed `name`, `menuPath`, and the JIPipeGraph `data`). Only what is mechanically derivable is filled: `modality` = the coarse data types flowing through the pattern (the registry `inputs[].dataType` / `outputs[].dataType` of its nodes, group order, deduped; `unknown` if not derivable — 2 doc(s): `chain-3-jipipe-compartment-output-jipipe-compartment-output-jipipe-compartment-output`, `conversion-jipipe-compartment-output-jipipe-compartment-output-jipipe-compartment-output`); `tags` = the top-level function branches of the pattern's nodes (parent-walk), deduped + sorted. `audience`, `biologicalContext` and `prerequisiteKnowledge` are the literal `tbd` — domain framing that cannot honestly be derived from generic algorithm node ids (most are ImageJ ops, not biology); `tbd` defers them to the LLM branch / a human.
- **Template `data` contract (R8b, verified against `JIPipeGraph.fromJson`/`nodesFromJson`/`edgesFromJson` and re-verified by the JUnit test):** `{"nodes": {<key>: nodeObj, ...}, "edges": [edgeObj, ...]}`. Per node: key = per-instance `n0`, `n1`, … (index in `exampleGraph.nodes`; duplicate registry ids are safe), nodeObj = `{"jipipe:node-info-id": <registered id>, "jipipe:graph-compartment": <ONE shared UUID string>, "jipipe:node:name": <example name>}`. Requirements found while reading the deserializer: the `nodes` key must be present (absent -> `fromJson` returns an EMPTY graph silently); `jipipe:node-info-id` must be a known registry id (unknown -> node skipped, soft); `jipipe:graph-compartment` is read via `.get(...).asText()` -> **NPE if the field is missing, it is REQUIRED** (a non-UUID string is tolerated: it falls back to the default compartment + a legacy id); `jipipe:node:name` sets the custom name that the deserializer's alias mechanism (`generateAliasIdFor`) keys off, so the `n<k>` map keys resolve the edges; edges carry `source-node`/`target-node` = those map keys and `source-slot` / `target-slot` = the registered slot names (the corpus `fromSlot` / `toSlot` came from the on-disk `slot-configuration.name` — a slot mismatch is a SOFT skip: the edge is dropped, not a crash). NO other field is required: the node's own `fromJson` guards every optional field and parameter deserialization reads `node.path(key)`, so parameters may be omitted entirely (minimal template = the three fields above).
- **6b formula:** score = support x breadth, where breadth = the number of distinct node ids across the pattern's `nodes[]` union (how many concrete algorithms it generalizes); rank by score desc, tie -> pattern id asc. All 185 patterns are template candidates (all are graph-shaped; sandwiches included).
- **6c** re-presents Task 7's cross-check (committed section 5) as the seeding hand-off for the LLM branch (1290-ai-llm-integration); numbers are parsed from section 5, not recomputed.

Mechanically derived from the pattern's role sequence + its `nodes[]` id lists.
